Theorem 217.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Every convergence analysis deformation of the nuclear_physics object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 549. The moduli space has dimension at most 871 with 2 connected components.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_549(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 549. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^871(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 217.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the nuclear_physics space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 543 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 386. QED.

Theorem 217.1001 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Let the nuclear_physics structure S satisfy the conditions of convergence analysis framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 625. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 791 and converges at rate O(1/n^2).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 625 is immediate from the convergence analysis property. For the inductive step, we decompose the nuclear_physics structure using the convergence analysis theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 791. Each subproblem has a solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
